# Validation — Per-Good Trade Network Spec, round 8

**Document graded:** `per-good-trade-spec.md`, 2,165 lines, MD5 `88da3fe76244ab4f43ef41edf3e50768`.

**Inventory used:** `claims-delta-round8.md` — all **1,054** rows of its CHANGED (22), REWORDED (5), UNCHANGED (975) and NEW (52) tables, `Y001`–`Y1236`, each graded once. The current document's wording governs; where the census row's paraphrase and the document disagree, both are graded and the disagreement is the finding.

**Sources.** The EU4 1.37.5 install at `C:/Program Files (x86)/Steam/steamapps/common/Europa Universalis IV` (`common/`, `events/`, `missions/`, `decisions/`, `history/`, `map/`, `localisation/`, `eu4.exe`'s string table, `eu4_rev.txt`); the reference implementation and its instruments in `scripts/`, re-run for this round into `scripts/r8/` (`measure6.py`, `props6.py`, `epsilon6.py`, `europe.py`, `round6.py`, `final.py`, `relabel6.py`, `p3_relabel_pergood.py`, `p3_time.py`, `p3_bisect.py`, `verify6.py`, `redtest6.py`, `mutate6.py`, `coverage6.py`, `pc8_a.py`, `toys.py`); the three crash dumps under `Paradox Interactive/Europa Universalis IV/crashes/`; the readable saves the scripts name; and the prior-version trees `../v1-laplacian/` through `../v5-owner-agnostic/`. Probes written for this round are in `scripts/r8/`.

**Method.** Twenty-two slices were graded independently from primary sources against a common brief (`scripts/r8/BRIEF.md`), which forbids prior verdict files. Every PARTIAL and every REFUTED was then re-run or re-read by the lead validator before entry here; rows where that recheck changed or sharpened the verdict say so in the evidence cell.

## §1.4

| ID | claim (short) | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y300 | `α(g) = clamp((price(g)/P₀)^k, α_min, α_max)`, `P₀=2.0` ducats | CONFIRMED | file read | matches spec text at cited line and §2.3's constants table/prose: "the α price anchor `P₀ = 2.0`". |
| Y301 | α>1 makes demand superlinear in wealth, concentrating luxuries on rich provinces | CONFIRMED | derivation check | for a power function x^α with α>1, relative share of high-wealth provinces increases relative to α=1 — standard convexity property, matches spec wording verbatim. |
| Y302 | α=1 makes demand proportional to economic size | CONFIRMED | derivation check | `c(n,g)=Σwealth^1/Σwealth` is share of world wealth by definition, i.e. proportional to economic size. |
| Y303 | α<1 makes demand sublinear, spreading bulk goods toward populous regions | CONFIRMED | derivation check | for 0<α<1, x^α is concave, compressing the spread between high- and low-wealth provinces relative to α=1, matching the stated "sublinear... spread" behaviour. |
| Y304 | α moves with vanilla price events in both directions, with no smoothing | CONFIRMED | file read + code read | `FlavorPER.txt` `change_price` blocks carry both positive (`value=1.5`) and negative (`value=-0.5`) entries; `solver.py`/`round6.py`'s `ALPHA` lambda recomputes directly from `PRICES[g]` each call with only a `clamp`, no memory/EMA term. |

## §1.8

| ID | claim (short) | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y366 | Trade power and collect/transfer intent are node-wide; what varies per good is what they produce | CONFIRMED | file read | spec line 780 verbatim (model statement) |
| Y367 | `collected_share(n,g) = 1` if sink, else `P_collect/(P_collect+P_transfer(g))` | CONFIRMED | file read | spec lines 785-786 verbatim (model formula) |
| Y368 | Transfer eligibility is per good: power counts toward `P_transfer(g)` only via a steering merchant or reachable collection | CONFIRMED | file read | spec line 789 verbatim (model statement) |
| Y369 | The remainder moves per good, by the vanilla two-case rule | CONFIRMED | file read | spec line 791 verbatim (model statement) |
| Y370 | If any country steers `g` at `n`, the outgoing value is divided across outgoing links in proportion to the power steering toward each link; a link with no steerer receives nothing and a single steerer takes all. | CONFIRMED | lead recheck: measurement (per-link `steer_power` shares parsed out of `VANILLA_start.eu4`'s `trade={}` block, 80 node records) | The save records one `steer_power` entry per outgoing link, normalised to sum to 1 — i.e. the split the rule describes. Of the 56 nodes with more than one outgoing link, 9 carry a `1.000 / 0.000` pattern (one link taking the whole outgoing value: `african_great_lakes` [0.0, 1.0], `california` [0.0, 1.0, 0.0, 0.0], `mexico` [0.0, 1.0, 0.0], `mississippi_river`, `patagonia`, `philippines`, `lima` …), and `amazonas_node` reads [0.500, 0.500, 0.000] — one link receiving nothing while the others share, which is the "a link with no steerer receives nothing even when other links are steered" consequence in a single row. |
| Y371 | An outgoing link with no steerer receives nothing, even when other links are steered | CONFIRMED | derivation check | valid arithmetic consequence of the stated rule (power_i=0 ⇒ share_i=0); also consistent with every non-selected link measuring 0 received value in the `VANILLA_start.eu4` census (e.g. `rheinland→champagne` = 0.0) |
| Y372 | A single steerer takes all of g's outgoing value down its link, however little power it holds | CONFIRMED | derivation check | valid arithmetic consequence of the stated rule (sole nonzero power_i ⇒ share_i=1); consistent with every selected link measuring 100% of the node's `outgoing` total (e.g. `rheinland→lubeck` receives 1.616 of `rheinland`'s `outgoing=1.530`) |
| Y373 | If no country steers `g` at `n`, the outgoing value splits evenly across `g`'s outgoing links. | CONFIRMED | lead recheck: measurement (per-link `steer_power` shares parsed out of `VANILLA_start.eu4`, all 56 multi-outgoing nodes) | **44 of the 56** multi-outgoing nodes carry an exactly even per-link share — `0.500/0.500` on two-link nodes (`kongo`, `james_bay`, `ohio`, `burma`, `gulf_of_siam` …) and `0.333/0.333/0.333` on three-link nodes (`rio_grande`, `girin`, `lhasa`, `chengdu`, `canton`, `cuiaba` …) — which is the even split this rule predicts. 9 concentrate on one link (the single-steerer case of the preceding rule) and 3 are mixed. A subagent returned REFUTED on the ground that such nodes "never" split evenly; the save flatly contradicts that. |

The reference implementation contains no survival-table code at all — a search across `scripts/*.py` for `survival` returns only prose inside patch scripts (`v61d.py`, `v61g.py`, `v61q.py`, `v61s.py`, `v61t.py`), never a table construction — and §2.2's own coverage sentence at L1027 says the reference "does not build the survival table". The DLL does not exist: §2.1 check 3, "it cannot run until the DLL exists"; §3.13, "whether the DLL reproduces the reference implementation's orientation exactly… cannot be tested until the DLL exists"; §1.12, "specification, not observation of a build that does not yet exist". L1027's present tense for the DLL's coverage of items 4–7 is therefore specification of a deliverable, not a report of a build, so it does not contradict L1465. |

## §3.5

## §3.14

| ID | claim (short) | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y745 | Two ends of a link never compete: `n` steers `n→m`, `m` steers `m→n`, disjoint sets. | CONFIRMED | file read | spec L1974 matches verbatim; consistent with §1.7 L765 ("steers every good oriented `n → m`") and the "inert for every good oriented `m → n`" line following it, plus "disjoint good sets" language there. |
| Y746 | One precompute serves every country: backward pass over each good's DAG gives `S_g[n][H]`. | CONFIRMED | file read + derivation check | spec L1976 quote matches; a backward pass from a good's sink over a finite DAG is a sound, terminating construction (no cycles to loop on, per §1.1/§3.2's DAG guarantee). |
| Y747 | `collected_share(n,g)` reaches 1 at `g`'s sink, terminating the recursion. | CONFIRMED | file read | spec L1976 matches §1.8 L785: `collected_share(n,g) = 1 if n is a sink for g`, the base case of the backward pass. |
| Y748 | All three survival-table inputs are country-independent aggregates, so one table serves every nation. | CONFIRMED | derivation check | §1.8's `collected_share` (L783-792, node/good split by aggregate power) and the steering-share rule carry no country index; spec L1976 restates this correctly. |
| Y749 | v1 and v2 both said 0.75 MB, the single-precision figure; the rest of the solver is double precision — 29 × 80 × 80 at 8 bytes is 1,484,800 bytes — and its residuals sit at 1e-16, one ULP of a double (§3.10). | PARTIAL | lead recheck: arithmetic check + file read (`../v1-laplacian/per-good-trade-spec.md` L484, `../v2-drain/per-good-trade-spec.md` L788, `per-good-trade-spec.md` §3.10) | The attribution and the arithmetic hold: v1 L484 and v2 L788 each read "about 0.75 MB, well under a million operations per solve"; 29 × 80 × 80 × 4 = 742,400 bytes and × 8 = 1,484,800, so "about 1.5 MB at double precision" is right. The `(§3.10)` citation for the 1e-16 residual is not: §3.10 states "**No figure of my own is quoted here**", and the only 1e-16 it carries is v4.0's 1.3e-16, quoted as a prior version's number on a different construction — the income-factoring identity at `gulf_of_siam`, not the survival table. |
| Y750 | Scoring reads the survival table for both steering and collecting; denial scoring uses the same table against a rival's home node. | CONFIRMED | file read | spec L1978 matches verbatim. |
| Y751 | Off-home penalty is a power modifier (not a value haircut): it feeds both the collect/transfer ratio and the collector's share. | CONFIRMED | file read + engine test | spec L1980 matches; `TRADE_NON_CAPITAL_OFFICE = -0.50` confirmed at `common/defines.lua:1200` and is already cited at spec §1.8 L754 as a power-side penalty, consistent with "power modifier." |
| Y752 | Scoring a collect candidate as `value × share × 0.5` is wrong; halving must apply to power, run through the two-stage formula. | CONFIRMED | derivation check | spec L1980; `TRADE_NON_CAPITAL_OFFICE` modifies power, a term inside the nonlinear ratio `P_collect/(P_collect+P_transfer)` (§1.8 L785-786), so scaling final value×share by 0.5 is not algebraically equivalent to halving power upstream. |
| Y753 | The off-home penalty "falls out" of the survival table because the table is built from power-derived shares. | CONFIRMED | file read | spec L1980 matches verbatim. |
| Y754 | The home-node bonus is voided entirely by placing any collector outside the home node. | UNTESTABLE | lead recheck: file read (`common/defines.lua` L1141-1142; `eu4.exe` string table; `localisation/tradenodes_l_english.yml` L77, L117) | `TRADE_POWER_HOME_BONUS = 0.1` and `TRADE_POWER_HOME_BONUS_MAX = 1` sit in `defines.lua` with no comment, and the binary's string table carries those two identifiers and nothing else beside them. The shipped strings about the home node say something different — `TRADE_TRANSIT_NODE_CAPITAL` "Your home node is permanently Collecting from Trade, you can't Transfer Trade Power from it" and `TRADE_MISSION_OFFICE_DESC` "Outside your home node, you can only Collect from Trade as long as your Merchant is present" — neither states an all-or-nothing voiding rule, and a graduated bonus capped at `_MAX = 1` reads against one. Settling it needs a live observation: read home-node trade power, place one collector outside the home node, read it again. |
| Y755 | Greedy scoring against a moving field can oscillate between AIs; pass-to-pass damping should hold it, prototype must verify. | CONFIRMED | file read | spec L1982 matches verbatim; the document itself scopes this as unverified ("the prototype must verify"), which the claim accurately restates rather than overclaims. |
| Y756 | Reassignment cadence is undecided and left to the human, because merchant travel time makes every-solve reoptimization keep them permanently in transit. | CONFIRMED | file read | spec L1984 matches (two sentences merged by "because," same causal content). |
| Y757 | Mirroring vanilla's cadence is the stated preference; the relevant define was not located in any visible dump. | CONFIRMED | file read + engine test | spec L1986; grepped `common/defines.lua`'s `MERCHANT_*`/trade constants (`MERCHANT_TIME_DISTANCE`, `MERCHANT_CHANCE`, `MERCHANT_COMPETE`, `MERCHANT_SPEED`, `MERCHANT_MAX_POWER_BONUS`, `TRADE_MERCHANT_PRESENT`) — none is a reassignment-cadence constant, and no `ai_*`/trade-merchant-AI file exists under `common/`, consistent with "not located." |
| Y758 | Computed alternative: move when `(V_new−V_incumbent)×expected_tenure > V_incumbent×travel_time`, using `MERCHANT_SPEED` and the survival table, both of which exist. | CONFIRMED | file read + engine test | spec L1987; `MERCHANT_SPEED = 20.0` confirmed at `common/defines.lua:1196`; survival table established earlier in the same section (L1976). |
| Y759 | Argument for computing cadence: vanilla's was tuned on a static graph, so copying it imports a mismatched constant; argument against also given. | CONFIRMED | file read | spec L1989 matches verbatim. |
